#### In firepower boost, Tatas to make US Javelin missiles in India

**In short:** Tata Advanced Systems on Monday signed a Memorandum of Understanding with Javelin Joint Venture of Lockheed Martin and Raytheon as the "prime partner" for commercial production of Javelin anti-tank guided missiles in India, days after India and the US inked a $45-million government-to-government contract to purchase 100 Javelin systems. The firms will explore a final assembly and integration facility in India; sub-assembly kits and guidance electronics will be made at Lockheed's Troy, Alabama, and Raytheon's Tucson, Arizona, facilities before shipment for final assembly.

#### Cases against me are medals on my chest: Rahul on U'khand FIR

**In short:** Opposition leader Rahul Gandhi said criminal cases filed against him were like "medals on my chest" after Uttarakhand Police registered an FIR accusing him of making alleged casteist remarks against Dalits following his August 29 press conference in support of Congress president Mallikarjun Kharge. The case followed a complaint by Amit Kumar, who alleged Gandhi's remarks were insulting to Dalits; the Congress said police had not acted against those involved in a 'shudhikaran' purification ceremony at Haldwani's Ramlila Maidan on August 10.

- **Purpose:** The case followed a complaint by Amit Kumar, who alleged Gandhi's remarks were insulting to Dalits; the Congress said police had not acted against those involved in a 'shudhikaran' purification ceremony at Haldwani's Ramlila Maidan on August 10.

- the Congress said police had not acted against those involved in a 'shudhikaran' purification ceremony at Haldwani's Ramlila Maidan on August 10.

#### Rift in Cong-TVK alliance over 2029 PM candidate

**In short:** Ruptures have appeared in the four-month-old alliance between the Congress and Tamil Nadu's ruling TVK over the prime ministerial candidate for the 2029 Lok Sabha elections, as TVK ministers push a 'Vijay as PM' narrative while the Congress has threatened to quit the Cabinet if the ruling party refuses to endorse Rahul Gandhi. Tourism Minister S Rajesh Kumar said two Congress ministers would immediately quit if TVK does not accept Rahul Gandhi as the 2029 PM candidate; the TVK government survives on the support of the Congress's five MLAs after failing to secure a majority in the 2026 Assembly election.

#### SC: K'taka already releasing mandated Cauvery river water

**In short:** The Supreme Court on Monday noted that Karnataka was already releasing the mandated quantity of Cauvery river water as it resumed hearing Tamil Nadu's plea for immediate release of its due share, listing the matter again on September 15. A bench of Justices Vikram Nath and Sandeep Mehta was told Karnataka released 9,888 cusec against the required 9,000 cusec on August 30 and over 11,000 cusec as of 8 am on Monday; senior advocate Shyam Divan said the state had declared severe drought in parts of the Cauvery basin on August 27.

The Supreme Court on Monday noted that Karnataka was already releasing the mandated quantity of Cauvery river water as it resumed hearing Tamil Nadu's plea for immediate release of its due share, listing the matter again on September 15. A bench of Justices Vikram Nath and Sandeep Mehta was told Karnataka released 9,888 cusec against the required 9,000 cusec on August 30 and over 11,000 cusec as of 8 am on Monday; senior advocate Shyam Divan said the state had declared severe drought in parts of the Cauvery basin on August 27. Tamil Nadu claimed a backlog of 17.604 tmcft while Karnataka contested the proportionate calculation method, stating total water ensured at the inter-state border exceeded the required amount by 30,885 cusecs or 2.6 tmcft up to August 31.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** SC: K'taka already releasing mandated Cauvery river water
- **Told:** The Supreme Court on Monday noted that Karnataka was already releasing the mandated quantity of Cauvery river water as it resumed hearing Tamil Nadu's plea for immediate release of its due share, listing the matter again on September 15.
- **Purpose:** Tamil Nadu claimed a backlog of 17.604 tmcft while Karnataka contested the proportionate calculation method, stating total water ensured at the inter-state border exceeded the required amount by 30,885 cusecs or 2.6 tmcft up to August 31.

**Important points**
- Tamil Nadu claimed a backlog of 17.604 tmcft while Karnataka contested the proportionate calculation method, stating total water ensured at the inter-state border exceeded the required amount by 30,885 cusecs or 2.6 tmcft up to August 31.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.7_

#### 'We have no idea where to look': Families await news of 85 missing

**In short:** Five days after flash floods swept Nepal's Rasuwa district on August 26, families of 85 tourists from Tamil Nadu — 36 en route to Kailash Mansarovar and 49 returning to Kathmandu — remain untraceable, while 199 of 305 pilgrims have returned and 21 were rescued. Relatives blocked the Chennai-Tiruchirapalli highway at Singaperumal Kovil on Sunday night demanding updates, saying the Immigration Centre at Gyirong on the Nepal-Tibet border was washed away and urging the Centre to coordinate with China to search the last known location.

#### Rakhi made it to Mumbai. Five days on, sister remains missing

**In short:** Siddharth Kuvavala in Mumbai is still waiting for news of his 48-year-old cousin Shital Chohan, an American citizen of Indian origin from Tennessee, who went missing during the August 26 Nepal-Tibet border floods while on her second Kailash Mansarovar pilgrimage with about 50 people organised by Kathmandu-based Himalayan Glacier. A tracker in her bag last placed her at flood-hit Pasang Lhamu on August 26; the Rakhi and sweets she sent from Kathmandu before the disaster now sit on Kuvavala's wrist as her husband and children in the US seek help from the US mission in Kathmandu.

#### Bike mechanic held for rape, murder of nurse in Telangana

**In short:** Hyderabad police on Monday arrested Mohammed Sanaullah, a 34-year-old bike mechanic from Bihar, for allegedly raping and murdering a 21-year-old nurse whose body was found in her room at a private hospital's staff quarters in Tukkuguda, Ranga Reddy district, on August 29. Police said he entered through a window, smothered her when she tried to raise an alarm and raped her while she was unconscious; Shamshabad DCP B Rajesh said he confessed during questioning, and Telangana Nurses Association members protested demanding better safety for healthcare workers.

#### Manipur Deputy CM, 8 MLAs threaten to walk out of govt.

**In short:** Manipur Deputy Chief Minister Losii Dikho and eight other Naga MLAs threatened to pull out of the BJP-led government unless the Centre acts against fellow Deputy Chief Minister Nemcha Kipgen, whom they accuse of colluding with a Kuki insurgent group, in an August 29 letter hand-delivered to Union Home Minister Amit Shah on Monday. They demanded arrest of Kuki-Zo leaders over the killing and dismemberment of six Naga civilians whose bodies were recovered on June 10, arrest of Thangboi Kipgen of the Kuki National Front (Presidential) faction, and abrogation of the 2008 Suspension of Operation agreement with 25 Kuki-Zo rebel groups.

They demanded arrest of Kuki-Zo leaders over the killing and dismemberment of six Naga civilians whose bodies were recovered on June 10, arrest of Thangboi Kipgen of the Kuki National Front (Presidential) faction, and abrogation of the 2008 Suspension of Operation agreement with 25 Kuki-Zo rebel groups. Five of the MLAs are from the Naga People’s Front, two from the BJP, one from the NPP, and one Independent.

#### Sadhguru pledges Rs 1 cr

**In short:** Spiritual leader Jaggi Vasudev, known as Sadhguru, pledged Rs 1 crore to Nepal's Prime Minister Disaster Relief Fund at the 'In Solidarity With Nepal' event in Kathmandu on Sunday following devastating flash floods along the Nepal-Tibet border. He proposed urgent creation of a five-nation Himalayan Board involving India, China, Pakistan, Nepal and Bhutan to jointly address natural disasters in the region.

#### Centre defers Census exercise in Manipur amid NRC demands

**In short:** The Union government has deferred Census operations in Manipur after a New Delhi review headed by Union Home Minister Amit Shah with Governor Ajay Kumar Bhalla, Chief Minister Yumnam Khemchand Singh and Union Home Secretary Govind Mohan, citing Meitei, Pangal and Naga demands to update the NRC before the Census. The High Court of Manipur, a Special Bench of Chief Justice M.

The Union government has deferred Census operations in Manipur after a New Delhi review headed by Union Home Minister Amit Shah with Governor Ajay Kumar Bhalla, Chief Minister Yumnam Khemchand Singh and Union Home Secretary Govind Mohan, citing Meitei, Pangal and Naga demands to update the NRC before the Census. The High Court of Manipur, a Special Bench of Chief Justice M. Sundar and Justice A. Guneswar Sharma, ordered the Census remain deferred until further orders; it had been scheduled to begin on September 1. Petitions by the Kangleipak Students’ Association and Shanta Nahakpam sought an NRC update using 1951 as the base year.

#### RAF denies RTI disclosure on pellet gun use

**In short:** The Rapid Action Force under the CRPF refused former Trinamool MP Saket Gokhale's RTI request for details of pellet-gun firing during the July 20 students' protest, saying the information relates to operational and security matters and does not fall under exceptions for human rights violations. Gokhale, whose first appeal also failed, said he would approach the Supreme Court, arguing CRPF claims use of pellet guns on protesters is not a human rights violation despite allegations of injuries to unarmed youth in the capital.

#### Guj youths beaten for 'reading Malala' threaten protest

**In short:** Members of Students and Youth Collective Ahmedabad and Gujarat Reads Anyway on Monday threatened protest if key suspects are not arrested within 72 hours and demanded a ban on Bajrang Dal from public life after saffron activists allegedly assaulted participants at an August 27 book-reading session in Ahmedabad, objecting to Malala Yousafzai's 'I Am Malala' among other titles. An FIR names Hiren Rabari and Jimit Rabari as alleged key suspects who remain at large; youth groups alleged Bajrang Dal members were present at the police station and that the complaint omitted important details stated by victims.

#### 2 shooters in singer's murder case killed in UP encounter

**In short:** Uttar Pradesh police on Monday shot dead alleged sharpshooters Sumit and Mohit in an encounter on Kairana Road in Shamli district while they were planning to escape to Sonipat in Haryana, five days after singer Anikt Baliyan was sprayed with bullets. Police said the duo opened fire when challenged; both died in hospital while two constables were injured.

#### Top court rejects plea on mandatory ethanol labelling at petrol pumps

**In short:** The Supreme Court on Monday dismissed a petition by Narendra Kumar Goswami seeking mandatory display of exact ethanol percentage on every petrol pump dispensing nozzle, fuel receipts and official websites, but granted him liberty to approach the high court concerned. A bench of Justices MM Sundresh and Prasanna B Varale found no merit after Attorney General R Venkataramani objected; Goswami argued consumers have a right to know fuel composition and sought a vehicle-compatibility advisory for E20 in the absence of alternatives.

#### SC seeks interrogation records of Abhishek Banerjee's aide

**In short:** The Supreme Court on Monday directed the West Bengal government to place on record details of the interrogation of Sumit Roy, personal assistant to Trinamool Congress MP Abhishek Banerjee, and extended his protection from arrest until September 7 in a Salboni land-grabbing case. Roy, challenging a Calcutta High Court denial of pre-arrest bail, alleged investigators questioned him for eight to nine hours daily over 11 days about his family background and Trinamool functioning rather than the alleged land transaction.

#### Goa drops plan to table anti-conversion bill after pushback from minority MLAs

**In short:** The Goa government on Monday decided not to introduce the Goa Prohibition of Unlawful Conversion of Religion Bill, 2026 during the ongoing monsoon Assembly session after Chief Minister Pramod Sawant met BJP MLAs Michael Lobo and Delilah Lobo and Independent legislator Aleixo Reginaldo Lourenco, who had vowed to oppose it. Lourenco said government data showed no instances of forced religious conversions in Goa; the bill, approved by the state Cabinet days earlier to prohibit conversions through force, coercion, allurement or fraudulent means including marriage, had also drawn opposition from the Congress, AAP and Christian organisations.

#### UP: 11 killed in blast at unlicensed cracker factory

**In short:** Eleven people, including two children, were killed and around 20 injured in a blast at an unlicensed cracker factory in Manauri Bazar, Kaushambi district, about 200 km from Lucknow, on Monday afternoon after a suspected fire triggered a series of explosions heard several kilometres away. Five to six adjacent houses collapsed, rescue teams from Prayagraj and the Army were pressed into service to remove debris, and Deputy Chief Minister Keshav Prasad Maurya said a probe would be conducted with stern action against the guilty.

Eleven people, including two children, were killed and around 20 injured in a blast at an unlicensed cracker factory in Manauri Bazar, Kaushambi district, about 200 km from Lucknow, on Monday afternoon after a suspected fire triggered a series of explosions heard several kilometres away. #### 3 Indians rescued by Nepali army in Rasuwa village

**In short:** Three Indian nationals were among four people rescued by the Nepali Army from Chilime village in flood-hit Rasuwa district, Indian Ambassador to Nepal Naveen Srivastava said on Monday. The Indian Embassy in Kathmandu is facilitating their return to India, while India has deployed specialised teams to assist Nepal in rescue operations and victim identification, including a 19-member forensics team in Kathmandu and a tunnel rescue team at hydropower sites in Chilime and Langtang.

#### HDFC Bank shares reverse gains on CEO succession buzz

**In short:** Shares of HDFC Bank Ltd erased early gains after CEO Sashidhar Jagdishan's decision to not seek a reappointment shifted investor focus to uncertainty over succession at India's most-valuable lender. The private lender's shares fell 0.6% after earlier rising as much as 2.7%, with Macquarie Group analyst Suresh Ganapathy saying leadership uncertainty may keep the stock in a narrow range as attention turns to finding a successor five months after part-time Chairman Atanu Chakraborty abruptly resigned.

#### ONGC to invest Rs 1L cr in deepwater push

**In short:** Oil and Natural Gas Corporation said on Monday it will invest Rs 1 lakh crore in deepwater exploration in the next five years, drilling seven deepwater oil and gas wells this year and targeting 10 next year, with plans to drill 87 deepwater wells over the period. Chairman and CEO Arun Kumar Singh said ONGC also plans to set up its international crude and products trading platform by the end of this fiscal year, with Dubai and Singapore likely to serve as the base.

#### 14 years on... still failing our daughters

**In short:** A 16-year-old from Mainpuri in Uttar Pradesh who boarded a bus in Greater Noida on the rainy night of August 4 was allegedly gang-raped by the conductor and driver and abandoned at Kashmere Gate, more than 40 km away, police said. The Class 11 student had mistakenly got off at Pari Chowk around 9 pm while heading to her cousin's home in Noida Sector 63; bus driver Kuldeep (39) and conductor Sandeep (26) have been arrested from a parking lot.

#### Sonia Gandhi memoir: Penguin cites 'particular issue'

**In short:** Penguin Random House India said on Monday there was no external pressure behind its decision not to publish Sonia Gandhi's memoir, citing an impasse over "a particular issue" during discussions on "Belonging: A Journey of Love" without disclosing the contentious matter. The clarification comes amid controversy after US-based Alfred A Knopf announced a November 10 release, while Congress leaders including Ashok Gehlot have alleged governmental pressure, with Gehlot claiming sections relating to Prime Minister Narendra Modi were targeted for removal.

#### India fastest growing economy with 7.8 pc in Q1

**In short:** India's economy grew at a faster-than-expected 7.8 per cent in the April–June quarter, slowing from 8.6 per cent in the previous quarter but remaining well above expectations and exceeding the Reserve Bank of India's forecast of 7 per cent. The growth showed resilience despite concerns that the war in Iran and global economic uncertainty could weigh on expansion and disrupt energy markets.

#### AIMIM worker stabbed to death by gang in Hyderabad

**In short:** An AIMIM worker, Mahboob Ali (in his mid-50s), was stabbed to death by a gang of five people near his residence at Vattepally in Hyderabad on Sunday night and died in hospital; police said the assailants were engaged by a relative of the deceased. AIMIM MLA Mohammed Mubeen visited the bereaved family and Falaknuma Police Station, urging strict action; a murder case was registered based on a complaint by the deceased's son and further investigation is underway.

#### Adopt 'guerrilla strategy', Jarange urges Marathas

**In short:** Maratha reservation activist Manoj Jarange on Monday urged community members to adopt a "guerrilla strategy," quietly move towards Mumbai and prepare for a major agitation if the government fails to implement demands by September 12, as his indefinite hunger strike at Antarwali Sarati in Jalna entered its third day. Jarange refused IV fluids on Sunday despite low blood sugar, claimed 58 lakh Kunbi genealogical records were found for Marathas, and said even if he died his body should be taken to Mumbai for the agitation.

#### AP CM inaugurates Veligonda irrigation project Phase-1

**In short:** Andhra Pradesh Chief Minister N Chandrababu Naidu on Monday inaugurated Phase-1 of the Veligonda irrigation project, releasing Krishna water into Nallamala Sagar to provide irrigation to 1.19 lakh acres and drinking water to four lakh people with 10.70 TMC entering the reservoir. The NDA government has spent more than Rs 2,400 crore on Veligonda in two years, disbursed Rs 768 crore to displaced families within 48 days, and the fully operational project is expected to irrigate 4.47 lakh acres across 30 mandals and benefit around 23 lakh people.

#### The cost of parliamentary silence

**In short:** Parliament has again been adjourned sine die without prorogation 17 days after the Monsoon Session ended, amid speculation the government could call a special sitting to revive the delimitation Bill defeated in April's Budget Session; repeated disruptions consumed time meant for debate, legislation and scrutiny. Joseph T R of Bengaluru writes that while a frequently cited PRS estimate puts the cost of running Parliament at ₹2.5 lakh per minute, that 2012 figure is outdated and the financial loss is only part of the story.

#### Bangladesh crime video falsely linked to WB, BJP workers

**In short:** A disturbing video showing recovery of a rape and murder victim's body in Bangladesh is being falsely shared on social media as an incident from West Bengal involving BJP workers, Boom established through reverse image search. The video was recorded in Shibchar, Madaripur district, Bangladesh, showing recovery of 28-year-old Shampa Khatun's body on August 19; Faruk Mollah was arrested on August 21, and the false claim comes amid clashes between ABVP and Left-backed student groups at Jadavpur University on August 20.

#### This professor runs a public library from her home

**In short:** Prof Madhu Khanna, an Oxford doctorate holder in Indology and former director at Jamia Millia Islamia, maintains the Tantra Foundation Library at Hari Bhari Farm in Chhattarpur, Delhi, actively inviting researchers in art, archaeology, Indology and religion unlike many private academic collections that remain locked. The 1970s modernist residence housing the library reflects early Chhattarpur farmhouse architecture and offers scholars access to her seminal work on Tantra and goddess-centred Shakta traditions.

#### Dispute over buffalo passage turns violent; one killed

**In short:** A dispute between two groups over taking a buffalo through a plot turned violent in Milawali village, Etah district, Uttar Pradesh, on Sunday evening, leaving eight people injured and Shyampal, 36, dead while being taken to a higher medical centre around 3 am on Monday. Police said Mukesh was returning from fields with his buffalo through Shyampal's plot when an objection led to a clash involving sticks, batons and an axe; four people from each side were injured.

#### 'Smash the patriarchy': Minister responds to remarks on confining women to homes

**In short:** Kerala Higher Education Minister Roji M John hit out at remarks seeking to confine women to their homes, ending a Facebook post with "SMASH THE PATRIARCHY," two days after Rahul Gandhi lashed out at the Manusmriti mindset. Though he did not name anyone, the post came a day after Islamic scholar Kanthapuram A P Aboobacker Musliyar said at a Kochi conference that bringing women onto public platforms causes "great destruction" and that Islam mandated purdah; Roji recalled Kerala's history of women's struggles including the Guruvayur Satyagraha and leaders such as Dakshayani Velayudhan and Accamma Cherian.

#### Govt considering easing FDI norms in defence

**In short:** The government is considering liberalisation of foreign direct investment norms in the defence sector, with the Department for Promotion of Industry and Internal Trade holding stakeholder consultations; some easing is under consideration, an official said on Monday. Currently FDI up to 74 per cent is permitted through the automatic route and beyond that through government approval where modern technology access is likely, subject to MHA security clearance; India attracted USD 32.29 million FDI in defence industries between April 2000 and March 2026.

#### Maruti Suzuki raises capex to Rs 77,500 cr

**In short:** Maruti Suzuki India has increased its capex outlay to Rs 77,500 crore for five years till FY31 for capacity expansion, new models and R&D, Managing Director and CEO Hisashi Takeuchi said at the AGM on Monday. Capex for FY26-27 alone is planned at Rs 14,000 crore, a 40 per cent jump from around Rs 10,000 crore last year, and he assured Maruti cars from production year 2008 are compatible with E20 fuel.

#### Zomato bans analogue dishes on its platform

**In short:** Food delivery aggregator Zomato on Monday announced a zero-tolerance policy on sale of analogue dishes including analogue cheese, paneer or other dairy products, reserving the right to delist non-compliant restaurants with immediate effect. Dishes declared as containing analogue dairy have been delisted, following renewed food safety focus after Maharashtra FDA crackdowns and FSSAI inspections in Delhi five-star hotels.

#### Centre's FY27 fiscal deficit touches 26.8 pc of full year target at end July

**In short:** The central government's fiscal deficit for 2026-27 stood at 26.8 per cent of the full-year target at the end of July, at Rs 4,55,144 crore in the April-July period against a target of 4.3 per cent of GDP or Rs 16.96 lakh crore, according to CGA data released on Monday. Net tax revenue was about Rs 8.45 lakh crore or 29.5 per cent of budget estimates and total expenditure was about Rs 17.62 lakh crore or 32.9 per cent of BE.

#### Ex-mill sugar prices fall 30%; retail rates same

**In short:** Ex-mill sugar prices fell nearly 30 per cent to Rs 47 per kg on Monday from a peak of Rs 67 per kg on August 18 following government interventions, but the drop has yet to filter to retail shelves as retailers hold stock bought at higher rates. Retail sugar was selling at Rs 64.23 per kg on Sunday against a wholesale rate of Rs 59.72 per kg, and an estimated 3-3.5 lakh tonnes of export-refined sugar is expected to enter the domestic market over the next two months.

**In short:** State-owned Indian Oil Corporation ramped up liquefied petroleum gas production by nearly 30 per cent and kept refineries operating above 100 per cent utilisation as Strait of Hormuz disruptions threatened India's energy supplies, chairman Arvinder Singh Sahney said at the 67th AGM on Monday. IOC diversified crude sourcing and secured alternative supplies as West Asia conflict disrupted global energy markets; India imports more than 88 per cent of its crude oil requirement.

#### Investments by PE/VC funds jump to $4.1 bln

**In short:** Private equity and venture capital funds' investments in India increased 3 per cent year-on-year to USD 4.1 billion in July, 52 per cent higher than USD 2.7 billion in June, according to an EY-IVCA report released on Monday. July recorded 10 large deals totalling USD 2.8 billion including Brookfield's USD 600 million bet in Lumara, with buyout investments accounting for USD 1.4 billion, a 176 per cent jump from July 2025.

#### Mahindra to manufacture parts for Airbus A320 plane

**In short:** Mahindra Aerostructures on Monday said it secured a contract from Airbus to manufacture and supply fuselage skins for A320 aircraft with delivery targeted for 2028, covering Sections 18 and 19 of the A320neo and A321XLR rear fuselage and tail sections. Components will be manufactured at Mahindra's Bengaluru facility and supplied to Airbus' Augsburg facility in Germany, expanding India's role in the global aerospace supply chain.

#### Chandra's dissenting lenders Canara Bank, Union Bank move NCLAT

**In short:** Dissenting lenders of Subhash Chandra on Monday moved NCLAT challenging the NCLT order approving a Rs 6.5 crore payment by the Essel Group chairman over creditor claims of about Rs 22,006.57 crore in his personal insolvency resolution process. Solicitor General Tushar Mehta, appearing for LIC Housing Finance, Canara Bank and Union Bank, sought urgent hearing saying the order would defeat the purpose of the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code; NCLAT listed the matter for Tuesday.

#### Bank of Baroda to recruit 2,482 Local Bank Officers

**In short:** Bank of Baroda will recruit 2,482 Local Bank Officers in Junior Management Grade Scale-I (JMGS-1) on a regular basis across 20 States and Union Territories, including Karnataka, with selected officers posted in the State they apply for during the first 12 years of service or until promotion to SMGS-IV, whichever is earlier. Online applications are open until September 7, 2026; candidates must be 21–30 as of September 1, 2026, graduates from a recognised university, fluent in the local language of the State they apply from, and a minimum of one year’s post-qualification experience as an officer in a Scheduled Commercial Bank is also desired.

#### Keralam CM orders SIT to probe Messi event row

**In short:** Keralam Chief Minister V.D. Satheesan directed the Home Department to constitute an SIT for an anti-corruption and suspected tax fraud inquiry into the previous LDF government’s endeavour to bring the 2021 FIFA World Cup-winning Argentina team and Lionel Messi for a friendly exhibition match at Jawaharlal Nehru Stadium in Kochi in August 2025.

Keralam Chief Minister V.D. Satheesan directed the Home Department to constitute an SIT for an anti-corruption and suspected tax fraud inquiry into the previous LDF government’s endeavour to bring the 2021 FIFA World Cup-winning Argentina team and Lionel Messi for a friendly exhibition match at Jawaharlal Nehru Stadium in Kochi in August 2025. A finance department inquiry found the sponsor, a news broadcasting company, wire-transferred ₹126 crore to a foreign entity in 2025, triggering a service tax obligation of ₹22.68 crore; he also ordered a probe of a suspected conspiracy to defraud the treasury.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Keralam CM orders SIT to probe Messi event row
- **Told:** Keralam Chief Minister V.D.
- **Purpose:** A finance department inquiry found the sponsor, a news broadcasting company, wire-transferred ₹126 crore to a foreign entity in 2025, triggering a service tax obligation of ₹22.68 crore; he also ordered a probe of a suspected conspiracy to defraud the treasury.

**Important points**
- A finance department inquiry found the sponsor, a news broadcasting company, wire-transferred ₹126 crore to a foreign entity in 2025, triggering a service tax obligation of ₹22.68 crore.
- he also ordered a probe of a suspected conspiracy to defraud the treasury.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.9_

#### V-dem’s democracy index rates India at its lowest since 1975

**In short:** Sambavi Parthasarathy and Gauri Singavarapu report that the UN CERD’s concern over large-scale voter exclusion, including of Muslims, in the Special Intensive Revision aligns with the V-Dem Institute’s latest electoral democracy index, released in March, which rates India at its lowest since 1975 Emergency rule. V-Dem listed India among countries “autocratising” slowly; scores on judicial accountability and corruption, Election Management Body autonomy, and impartial administration are at their lowest in 50 years.

#### Efforts to improve life of Sikkim Lepchas

**In short:** A fifty-years-ago reprint dated Gangtok, August 31, 1976, reported that a detailed land survey had started in Dzongu, North Sikkim, as a step toward land reforms for Lepchas, then about 15% of the State’s population. Elders at a meeting at Tingbhum in Dzongu passed a resolution favouring changes in their way of life, especially expenses on marriage.

#### After hottest August, bleak outlook for September rain

**In short:** The IMD on Monday forecast September rainfall at least 9% short of the long-period average of 167.9 mm after India recorded the hottest August since 1901, with mean temperature 28.01 degrees Celsius, about 0.67 degree above normal, and the highest average minimum of 24.36 degrees Celsius. As of August 31, cumulative monsoon rainfall was about 14% below normal; August rain was 213.3 mm, the seventh-lowest since 2001.

The IMD on Monday forecast September rainfall at least 9% short of the long-period average of 167.9 mm after India recorded the hottest August since 1901, with mean temperature 28.01 degrees Celsius, about 0.67 degree above normal, and the highest average minimum of 24.36 degrees Celsius. As of August 31, cumulative monsoon rainfall was about 14% below normal; August rain was 213.3 mm, the seventh-lowest since 2001. IMD Director-General Mrutyunjay Mohapatra linked the pattern to El Niño, which models indicate is likely to intensify for the rest of the 2026 southwest monsoon.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** After hottest August, bleak outlook for September rain
- **Told:** The IMD on Monday forecast September rainfall at least 9% short of the long-period average of 167.9 mm after India recorded the hottest August since 1901, with mean temperature 28.01 degrees Celsius, about 0.67 degree above normal, and the highest average minimum of 24.36 degrees Celsius.
- **Purpose:** IMD Director-General Mrutyunjay Mohapatra linked the pattern to El Niño, which models indicate is likely to intensify for the rest of the 2026 southwest monsoon.

**In short:** The NCLT on Monday formed a five-member Bench, the first in its history, to decide media baron Subhash Chandra’s repayment plan in a personal insolvency case involving claims of more than ₹22,000 crore after a Division Bench failed to reach a majority on a proposed ₹6.5 crore repayment plan. #### India’s GDP growth at 7.8% in Q1, slower than last quarter but quicker than last year

**In short:** India’s GDP grew 7.8% in April–June 2026 (Q1 FY27), quicker than 6.9% in Q1 last year but slower than 8.6% in January–March 2026; Finance Minister Nirmala Sitharaman said nominal GDP grew 10.3% and real GVA 8.2%. Manufacturing grew 9.2%, construction 7.7%, utilities 8.9%, services 10%, and financial, real estate, IT and professional services 12.1%; agriculture slowed to 3.6% and mining contracted 2.4%.

India’s GDP grew 7.8% in April–June 2026 (Q1 FY27), quicker than 6.9% in Q1 last year but slower than 8.6% in January–March 2026; Finance Minister Nirmala Sitharaman said nominal GDP grew 10.3% and real GVA 8.2%. Manufacturing grew 9.2%, construction 7.7%, utilities 8.9%, services 10%, and financial, real estate, IT and professional services 12.1%; agriculture slowed to 3.6% and mining contracted 2.4%. Gross Fixed Capital Formation rose to 34.3% of GDP in nominal terms from 31.4% last year and grew 20.4% in the quarter.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** India’s GDP growth at 7.8% in Q1, slower than last quarter but quicker than last year
- **Told:** India’s GDP grew 7.8% in April–June 2026 (Q1 FY27), quicker than 6.9% in Q1 last year but slower than 8.6% in January–March 2026; Finance Minister Nirmala Sitharaman said nominal GDP grew 10.3% and real GVA 8.2%.
- **Purpose:** Gross Fixed Capital Formation rose to 34.3% of GDP in nominal terms from 31.4% last year and grew 20.4% in the quarter.

**Important points**
- Gross Fixed Capital Formation rose to 34.3% of GDP in nominal terms from 31.4% last year and grew 20.4% in the quarter.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.15_

#### What two districts can teach us about dealing with rural waste

**In short:** Mahima Jain reports that India produces 1.7 lakh tonnes of waste a day (CPCB), with 22% landfilled and 17% unaccounted for, while UNEP estimates 2.7 billion people lack waste collection, 2 billion of them rural. Majuli transferred a tonne of waste across the Brahmaputra on April 30, 2025, and between April 2025 and May 2026 moved 16.78 tonnes earning ₹1.47 lakh; since 2024 it has collected 82.4 tonnes.

Mahima Jain reports that India produces 1.7 lakh tonnes of waste a day (CPCB), with 22% landfilled and 17% unaccounted for, while UNEP estimates 2.7 billion people lack waste collection, 2 billion of them rural. Majuli transferred a tonne of waste across the Brahmaputra on April 30, 2025, and between April 2025 and May 2026 moved 16.78 tonnes earning ₹1.47 lakh; since 2024 it has collected 82.4 tonnes. In Tawang, 26 project villages organised more than 150 Swachchata Divas and collected 30 tonnes, selling more than 20 tonnes to recyclers for ₹3.53 lakh, with households paying a ₹50 monthly fee.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** What two districts can teach us about dealing with rural waste
- **Told:** Mahima Jain reports that India produces 1.7 lakh tonnes of waste a day (CPCB), with 22% landfilled and 17% unaccounted for, while UNEP estimates 2.7 billion people lack waste collection, 2 billion of them rural.

#### What goes on behind the screen when you pay through UPI?

**In short:** Vasudevan Mukunth explains that UPI, operated by NPCI, processed 2,366 crore transactions in July 2026 worth nearly ₹30 lakh crore (around 7,600 per second); in 2025-26 it handled 24,160 crore transactions worth ₹314 lakh crore, 85% of India’s digital payments by volume and 49% of real-time payments worldwide in 2025 according to the IMF. Raghuram Rajan piloted UPI in April 2016 with 21 banks; it crossed 100 crore transactions a month in October 2019.

### International

#### Move from 'endless war': PM tells Putin

**In short:** Prime Minister Narendra Modi on Monday urged Russian President Vladimir Putin to move away from the "endless war" with Ukraine towards an "end to the war", and assured Iranian President Massoud Pezeshkian of India's support to resolve Iran's conflict with the United States and Israel, during separate meetings on the sidelines of the Shanghai Cooperation Organisation summit in Bishkek. Modi said every day spent in war sets humanity back and that a peaceful resolution was essential; Putin replied, "We are moving in this direction," as the meetings came less than a fortnight before Modi would host both leaders at the BRICS summit in New Delhi.

#### Nepal flood toll 900; over 4,200 still missing

**In short:** The toll in devastating flash floods and mudslides caused by a glacial collapse in Nepal has gone over 900, with more than 4,200 people reported missing, including 590 foreign nationals and 99 security personnel. Triggered by a wave of ice, rocks, and debris along the Bhote Koshi River, the disaster has affected central and northern regions, leaving at least 933 workers trapped across six hydropower projects; Prime Minister Balendra Shah called it an "unimaginable and heartbreaking" calamity.

#### Saudi Arabia, Turkey, Pak agree to defence pact secretariat

**In short:** Saudi Arabia, Turkey and Pakistan agreed to establish a secretariat in Saudi Arabia under their Mecca joint defence agreement, which will initially be headed by a Pakistani secretary-general for three years, Saudi Arabia's foreign ministry said on Monday. Turkey's Foreign Minister Hakan Fidan stressed after the first ministerial meeting in Istanbul that the regional defence pact was not aimed at any particular country and was open to others, including Egypt, committing the three nations to mutual defence if any of them is attacked.

#### 100-yr-old temple demolished

**In short:** A more than century-old Hindu temple has been demolished, reportedly to incorporate its land into a madrassa in Pakistan's Punjab province, with local residents, minority groups and a political party saying religious zealots demolished the shrine on August 21. Occupying approximately 1,000 square metres in Siraj village in Narowal, about 130 km from Lahore, the 100- to 125-year-old temple was torn down by members of the banned far-right Tehreek-i-Labbaik Pakistan, while the government claimed the structure collapsed due to torrential rains.

#### Kenya strike delays flights

**In short:** A workers' strike has disrupted air traffic at Kenya's main airport in Nairobi, leaving many travellers stranded after flights were delayed or cancelled, the Kenya Airports Authority said on Monday. Members of the Kenya Aviation Workers Union are protesting alleged unfair staff hiring practices, poor compensation and proposed changes to the management of Jomo Kenyatta International Airport.

#### Indian charged for murder

**In short:** A 25-year-old Indian national, Muruganathan Kathirvel, was on Monday charged with murder in Singapore for allegedly killing Indian-origin Raj Maheshkumar on August 29 by slashing his neck with a chopper. Police said they received a call for assistance at about 10.10 pm on August 29 in an industrial zone on Singapore's west coast, where the 29-year-old victim was found lying motionless on the road and pronounced dead at the scene.

#### UN committee says countries must consider reparations for slave trade

**In short:** A UN committee said countries are legally obliged to consider reparations for the transatlantic slave trade and take other measures to address the enduring legacy of racial discrimination, in guidance published Monday by the Committee on the Elimination of Racial Discrimination. The document, describing a "paradigm shift" away from debates over historical responsibility, states that obligations arise from the 1965 convention on racial discrimination and urges "transformative" measures including opening archives, revising public memorials and establishing independent truth commissions, noting at least 12.5 million Africans were taken and sold between the 15th and 19th centuries.

#### North Korea vows to strengthen nuclear force

**In short:** North Korea said on Monday the United States' hostile policy remained unchanged and vowed to continue strengthening its nuclear force, rejecting repeated U.S. calls for its denuclearisation.

North Korea said on Monday the United States' hostile policy remained unchanged and vowed to continue strengthening its nuclear force, rejecting repeated U.S. calls for its denuclearisation. In a statement carried by state media KCNA, a Foreign Ministry spokesperson accused Washington of maintaining a confrontational stance through its commitment to the "complete denuclearisation" of North Korea, criticism of the country's human rights record and planned joint military exercises with South Korea and Japan.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** North Korea vows to strengthen nuclear force
- **Told:** North Korea said on Monday the United States' hostile policy remained unchanged and vowed to continue strengthening its nuclear force, rejecting repeated U.S.
- **Purpose:** In a statement carried by state media KCNA, a Foreign Ministry spokesperson accused Washington of maintaining a confrontational stance through its commitment to the "complete denuclearisation" of North Korea, criticism of the country's human rights record and planned joint military exercises with South Korea and Japan.

**Important points**
- In a statement carried by state media KCNA, a Foreign Ministry spokesperson accused Washington of maintaining a confrontational stance through its commitment to the "complete denuclearisation" of North Korea, criticism of the country's human rights record and planned joint military exercises with South Korea and Japan.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.12_

#### Trump vows 'hard' response after fresh exchange of fire with Iran

**In short:** Donald Trump threatened to hit Iran "hard" after the two foes traded fire for the first time in over a month, with the US saying it carried out strikes on Iranian rocket launchers on the island of Larak in the Strait of Hormuz and Iran retaliating with missile and drone attacks aimed at Jordan and the United Arab Emirates. State media reported two people were killed in the US attack on Larak, while President Masoud Pezeshkian later called for dialogue, saying continuing the war was neither in Iran's interest nor in the interest of the region.

#### Congo Ebola outbreak tops 6,000 cases

**In short:** Congo's authorities said Monday that the fastest-growing Ebola outbreak in history has topped 6,000 cases, with 2,911 deaths, as the outbreak in eastern Congo spreads under extremely difficult conditions fuelled by insecurity, displacement, a health workers' strike and intense population movements.

#### Airline defends dangerous stunt

**In short:** Regional carrier Airlink defended a dramatic low-altitude flypast of two commercial jets over Cape Town's DHL Stadium ahead of a South Africa-New Zealand rugby match, saying the manoeuvre was meticulously planned with aviation authorities and each aircraft was under three senior captains operating within approved safety parameters. Critics called the spectacle a reckless hazard near a capacity crowd while supporters praised pilot precision.

#### Indonesia's Sinabung volcano erupts

**In short:** Mount Sinabung in Indonesia erupted on Monday, spewing a column of black ash and smoke 3,500 metres from the summit over Sumatra island, with no immediate reports of casualties or evacuations but residents advised to refrain from activities within 6 kilometres of the summit. Officials raised the volcano alert level to "Watch," the second-highest level, as several villages in Karo district were covered in grey ash and masks were distributed.

#### Policeman killed in Pak IED explosion

**In short:** One policeman was killed and six injured when their vehicle was targeted with an improvised explosive device in Khyber Pakhtunkhwa, Pakistan, on Monday while travelling from Banda Police Station towards Dargah in Karak district for a search operation. Chief Minister Sohail Afridi condemned the attack as cowardly and a search operation was launched to trace the perpetrators.

#### Nepal tunnel workers recount horror and ordeal

**In short:** Workers from Nepal's flood-hit hydropower projects described desperate escapes, including Shiva Giri and colleagues who trekked 12 hours through forest after the Mailung Hydropower Project was swamped, as rescue teams try to trace 933 workers out of contact since August 26 with more than 600 believed stranded inside tunnels. So far 279 people have been rescued from tunnels and expert teams from India and China have joined Nepal's efforts.

#### Modi meets Iranian Prez, calls for end to war

**In short:** Prime Minister Narendra Modi told Iranian President Masoud Pezeshkian on the sidelines of the SCO summit in Bishkek on Monday that India wants to expand and diversify trade with Iran and called for resolution of the West Asia conflict through dialogue and diplomacy. The MEA said Modi expressed concern at escalating tensions, reiterated support for efforts ensuring lasting peace, and emphasised that civilians, civilian infrastructure and commercial shipping must not be harmed.

#### Lawyer, activist & aid giver win this year's Magsaysay awards

**In short:** Singaporean lawyer Tommy Koh, Myanmar activist Bo Kyi and Bangladeshi aid worker Runa Khan were named winners of the 2026 Ramon Magsaysay Awards on Monday. Koh was recognised for presiding over the 1982 UN Convention on the Law of the Sea conference, Bo Kyi for documenting human rights atrocities after seven years in prison, and Runa Khan for aid work that now employs more than 7,500 people through hospital ships and land-based hospitals.

#### 15 missing in flash floods in Grand Canyon

**In short:** A major flash flood at the Grand Canyon left one person dead and about 15 others missing or unaccounted for, with scores of visitors evacuated and the only water pipeline for tourists and residents destroyed. Emergency water restrictions began Monday preventing overnight stays at lodges, and the body of a 46-year-old man was recovered Sunday evening near Crystal Rapids along the Colorado River.

#### Nashville says final farewell to Dolly Parton

**In short:** US country music legend Dolly Parton was laid to rest in a private family ceremony at Nashville's Woodlawn Memorial Park beside her late husband Carl Dean after her death at 80 following a brief battle with cancer. Family members including nephew Bryan Seaver gathered for the intimate service after the music community rallied in grief and celebration over the weekend.

#### Ensure peace, freedom of navigation, PM urges Iran

**In short:** In the first top-level Iran–India meeting since the war on Iran began in February, Prime Minister Narendra Modi met Iranian President Masoud Pezeshkian on Monday in Bishkek, Kyrgyzstan, on the sidelines of the SCO summit and urged him to ensure peace and stability in West Asia and freedom of navigation in the Strait of Hormuz; the MEA said he called for freedom of navigation and commerce and that civilians and civilian infrastructure, including commercial shipping and seafarers, must not be harmed. Mr.

In the first top-level Iran–India meeting since the war on Iran began in February, Prime Minister Narendra Modi met Iranian President Masoud Pezeshkian on Monday in Bishkek, Kyrgyzstan, on the sidelines of the SCO summit and urged him to ensure peace and stability in West Asia and freedom of navigation in the Strait of Hormuz; the MEA said he called for freedom of navigation and commerce and that civilians and civilian infrastructure, including commercial shipping and seafarers, must not be harmed. Mr. Pezeshkian blamed Israel and the U.S. for the conflict and asked Mr. Modi to use India’s extensive contacts with various parties to help the dialogue process. After the meeting Mr. Modi said on X he reiterated commitment to strengthening India’s long-standing friendship with Iran, expanding and diversifying the trade basket, and that India will continue to support efforts for lasting peace, and that he looked forward to welcoming Mr. Pezeshkian to India for the upcoming BRICS summit.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Ensure peace, freedom of navigation, PM urges Iran
- **Told:** In the first top-level Iran–India meeting since the war on Iran began in February, Prime Minister Narendra Modi met Iranian President Masoud Pezeshkian on Monday in Bishkek, Kyrgyzstan, on the sidelines of the SCO summit and urged him to ensure peace and stability in West Asia and freedom of navigation in the Strait of Hormuz; the MEA said he called for freedom of navigation and commerce and that civilians and civilian infrastructure, including commercial shipping and seafarers, must not be harmed.
- **Purpose:** Pezeshkian to India for the upcoming BRICS summit.

**Important points**
- Pezeshkian blamed Israel and the U.S.
- for the conflict and asked Mr.
- Modi to use India’s extensive contacts with various parties to help the dialogue process.
- After the meeting Mr.
- Modi said on X he reiterated commitment to strengthening India’s long-standing friendship with Iran, expanding and diversifying the trade basket, and that India will continue to support efforts for lasting peace, and that he looked forward to welcoming Mr.
- Pezeshkian to India for the upcoming BRICS summit.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.5_

#### Marconi’s new invention

**In short:** A hundred-years-ago reprint from an August 29, 1926 London cable said Signor Marconi had experimented with a special loud speaker giving great amplification, believing the voice could carry twenty miles. He was leaving for Italy in the yacht Electra on September 1 to test beam strength between England and Canada and England and South Africa.

#### UN panel presents legal case for broad slavery reparations

**In short:** The UN Committee on the Elimination of Racial Discrimination said on Monday that states parties must implement comprehensive reparatory measures for people of African descent covering monetary, non-monetary and structural remedies for harms from the transatlantic African slave trade. At least 12.5 million Africans were taken and sold between the 15th and 19th centuries; committee member Pela Boker-Wilson said the findings carry authoritative weight though they are not legally binding.

The UN Committee on the Elimination of Racial Discrimination said on Monday that states parties must implement comprehensive reparatory measures for people of African descent covering monetary, non-monetary and structural remedies for harms from the transatlantic African slave trade. #### Russia, Israel behind disinformation on Ceuta crisis: Sanchez

**In short:** Spanish Prime Minister Pedro Sanchez said on Monday there were signs Russia and Israel had spread disinformation over Spain’s migration policy that triggered last month’s rush of migrants into Ceuta, but no evidence of Moroccan involvement. Over 72,000 migrants irregularly crossed from Morocco into Ceuta between July 30 and 31, with at least 100 people dying in the attempt according to local authorities; about 5,000 have stayed in Ceuta.

### Sports

#### Final whistle: Messi calls time on glittering career

**In short:** After mesmerising the football world for over two decades and winning nearly every accolade, Lionel Messi announced he was done playing for Argentina, following the team's 1-0 loss to Spain in the July FIFA World Cup Final where the 39-year-old scored eight goals. The decision became clearer after his father Jorge Messi died on August 7; Messi had briefly retired after Argentina's 2016 Copa America final loss to Chile but reversed that decision and went on to win two Copa Americas and the 2022 FIFA World Cup in Qatar.

**Important points**
- Lionel Messi announced he was done playing for Argentina, following the team's 1-0 loss to Spain in the July FIFA World Cup Final where the 39-year-old scored eight goals. The decision became clearer after his father Jorge Messi died on August 7.
- Messi had briefly retired after Argentina's 2016 Copa America final loss to Chile but reversed that decision and went on to win two Copa Americas and the 2022 FIFA World Cup in Qatar.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.1_

Perfect Attitude, ridden by Antony Raj, won the Div-II Krishnaswamy Memorial Trophy in 1:13.38.

#### Asian Games-bound horse infected, sparks testing row

**In short:** India will be down one rider-horse combination in three-day eventing at the 2026 Asian Games after Captain Aahan Kumar's horse Bolivar Gio Granno tested positive for Piroplasmosis, with reserve rider Pradeep Jhangam alleging the Equestrian Federation of India's ad-hoc committee failed to send his horse's blood sample required by August 17. Lt Col Sandeep Dewan of the ad-hoc panel disputed the allegations, saying selected riders based abroad had given undertakings that their horses were fit and that mandatory pre-quarantine testing was conducted by the official Asian Games transporter, with Bolivar Gio Granno's positive result communicated on the evening of August 25.

#### Karnataka youngsters dominate Day 1

**In short:** Karnataka's young tennis players were at the centre of an action-packed opening round at the KSLTA World Tennis Tour Junior J30 Bengaluru at the SM Krishna Tennis Stadium on Monday, with closely fought contests across boys' and girls' singles. Notable results included third seed Niyanth Badri Narayanan (USA) beating Manan Rai 6-4, 6-1, top seed Shaivi Gaurav Dalal defeating Dhaanvi D Kanjithanda 6-2, 6-1, and third seed Disha Kumar edging Sidhhi Pandey 6-1, 6-7 (3), 7-5.

#### Ailing Djokovic makes shock exit

**In short:** Novak Djokovic suffered his earliest Grand Slam exit in 20 years on Sunday, beaten by Argentina's Mariano Navone 7-6 (7/5), 5-7, 4-6, 6-2, 6-1 in the US Open first round, ending his run of 78 Grand Slam first-round victories dating back to 2006. The 39-year-old vomited on court and struggled with late-match cramps and back pain, saying he has carried such physical problems "pretty much every match this year," while 46-year-old Venus Williams also lost in straight sets to compatriot Sofia Kenin 6-2, 7-6 (6).

#### Sooryavanshi puts on a show in red ball too

**In short:** Fifteen-year-old Vaibhav Sooryavanshi blasted 92 off 81 balls to become the youngest batter to score a Duleep Trophy half-century, breaking a 57-year-old record held by the late Laxman Singh, as East Zone were bowled out for 266—identical to Central Zone's first-innings total—in the semifinal in Bengaluru on Monday. Left-arm spinner Harsh Dubey took 4 for 66 as East lost 10 wickets for 105 runs after Sooryavanshi and Abhimanyu Easwaran's 161-run opening stand, leaving Central at 45 for three at stumps in their second essay.

#### Persistence pays off for South

**In short:** Captain Tilak Varma made an unbeaten 106 off 225 balls and Shreyas Gopal scored 87 as South Zone finished at 285 for nine on day two of the Duleep Trophy semifinal against North Zone in Bengaluru, taking a first-innings lead of 90 runs after North were bowled out for 195. Gurnoor Brar took 4 for 70 as South recovered from 80 for five through a 175-run sixth-wicket stand between Tilak and Gopal, though South lost three wickets for two runs in the last 30 minutes of play.

#### New playing conditions for K Thimmappiah Memorial

**In short:** The Karnataka State Cricket Association has made three significant rule changes to its flagship four-day Dr (Capt) K Thimmappiah memorial tournament kicking off across Bengaluru and Mysuru on Tuesday to encourage teams to pursue victory rather than play for a draw. Each team is limited to 120 overs in the first innings and 60 in the second—with half of any unused first-innings overs added to the second—teams can take the new ball after 70 overs and must take it after 90, and fielding sides may have no more than three fielders outside the inner circle after completing one-third of their maximum overs in the second innings.

#### Shafali calls for assessment after batting implosion

**In short:** India opener Shafali Verma said the team's batting unit will assess its shortcomings on turners despite opening the Women's Asia Cup with a 94-run victory over Thailand in Dubai, where India slumped from 91 for one to 159 for nine after her 64 off 36 balls. Shafali said the track offered hold and turn with the older ball and credited Thailand's bowlers, acknowledging she could have played the ball better after it gripped and arrived a little late.

#### Pak set to send Rizwan, Imam back home

**In short:** Pakistan are likely to send wicketkeeper-batter Mohammad Rizwan and opening batter Imam-Ul-Haq back home from their tour of England, where the team has endured heavy defeats in the first two Tests by an innings and 103 runs at Leeds and by 194 runs at Lord's. Geo News reported the decision following a high-level meeting of Pakistan Cricket Board officials including chairman Mohsin Naqvi, COO Sumair Ahmed and head coach for limited-overs cricket Mike Hesson.

#### 5-star room shortage hits Asiad cricket: official

**In short:** The Asian Cricket Council has heard concerns over a lack of five-star hotel accommodation for players at the upcoming Asian Games in Japan, Sri Lanka Cricket secretary Prakash Schaffter said on Monday. Men's and women's T20 cricket will be played at Japan's newly-built cricket ground in Nisshin starting September 17, and member states have agreed to send representatives to examine grounds and logistical arrangements while hoping the tournament will still go ahead.

#### Nivaan, Krupa wear U-15 chess title

**In short:** Nivaan Raghavendra and Krupa S Ukkali won the open and girls titles at the Janamitra Karnataka Chess U-15 Championships in Hassan District, each scoring seven points. Nivaan took first place ahead of Yeshas H, Indrajit Majumdar and Venkata Naga K M in a four-way tie, while Krupa edged Simran Nadaf and Indusheetala by 0.5 points, with the top four from both categories qualifying for the U-15 National Championships.

**In short:** Real Madrid coach Jose Mourinho called for patience with Ivory Coast winger Yan Diomande, a reported $146 million signing from Leipzig who has made three substitute appearances without replicating his Bundesliga or World Cup form. Mourinho said Diomande needs to go "little by little" after Madrid's 4-0 win over Malaga, their third from three league games this season.

#### Venus Williams bows out of her 26th US Open

**In short:** Venus Williams bowed out of her 26th US Open singles appearance early Monday, losing 6-2, 7-6 (6) to fellow American wild card Sofia Kenin after not getting on court until midnight in what could be her final singles match at a Grand Slam. #### Vaibhav slams 92 as East Zone battle Central

**In short:** Fifteen-year-old Vaibhav Sooryavanshi scored 92 off 81 balls to become the youngest batter to score a fifty in the Duleep Trophy, but East Zone were bowled out for 266—identical to Central Zone's first-innings total—in the semifinal in Bengaluru on Monday. Left-arm spinner Harsh Dubey took 4 for 66 as East lost 10 wickets for 105 runs in a collapse after Sooryavanshi and Abhimanyu Easwaran's 161-run opening stand, leaving Central at 45 for three at stumps.

**In short:** Ollie Robinson and Jofra Archer took 24 of Pakistan's 38 possible wickets in harness together for the first time in Test cricket as England clinched the series 2-0 against Pakistan at Lord's on Sunday with a day and a game to spare. **In short:** Lionel Messi, 39, ended his Argentina career after 125 goals in 207 matches, the national records for appearances and goals, writing that the decision still hurts but the time had come; he drafted the letter on July 21, two days after losing the World Cup final to Spain, and delayed publishing it because his father Jorge Messi died in Rosario on August 8. #### UNDP internships: Gateways to a global career

**In short:** For Indian students interested in international development, public policy, economics, sustainability, communications or global affairs, a UNDP internship can offer practical exposure across offices including the Washington Representation Office, which currently offers General and Communications & Outreach internships. UNDP internships are generally open to final-year bachelor's students, master's students and recent graduates who begin within one year of graduation, with applications requiring tailored cover letters and CVs connecting skills to each role; students can also explore opportunities with UNDP India covering sustainable development, climate resilience and the Sustainable Development Goals.

#### Nutrition guide for healthy hair and regrowth

**In short:** Hair is primarily composed of keratin and requires adequate protein, iron, omega-3 fatty acids, B-vitamins, zinc, selenium and vitamins C and E for follicular health; deficiency can cause premature resting phase and increased shedding. The article recommends eggs, berries, spinach, fatty fish, nuts and sweet potatoes while advising against refined sugars, crash dieting and excessive alcohol or caffeine, noting consistent nutritional changes generally take 3 to 6 months to show results.

#### Understanding conjunctivitis or eye flu

**In short:** Conjunctivitis, commonly called eye flu or pink eye, is inflammation of the conjunctiva causing dilated blood vessels and a pink appearance, with types including viral, bacterial, allergic and chemical or irritant forms. Viral and bacterial forms are highly contagious while allergic conjunctivitis is not; management ranges from supportive care and antibiotics where appropriate to avoiding triggers and immediate flushing for chemical exposure, with medical evaluation advised for severe pain, light sensitivity, blurred vision or symptoms persisting beyond five days.

#### Controlling anger before it controls you

**In short:** Unmanaged temper stems from an overactive fight-or-flight response and can erode relationships, health and peace of mind; learning to control anger means shifting from impulsive reaction to intentional response. Immediate strategies include a 5-to-10-second pause, controlled box breathing, and physical disengagement from heated conversations, while long-term techniques involve trigger mapping, cognitive reframing and assertive "I" statements, with professional support such as CBT recommended for persistent outbursts.

#### 1. NIT-K associate prof. Shashi Bhushan receives ECSI award

**In short:** Shashi Bhushan Arya, associate professor in the Department of Metallurgical and Materials Engineering at NIT-K Surathkal, received the Electrochemical Society of India’s S.K. Seshadri Memorial Mascot National Award–2026, with a cash prize of ₹25,000, for work on industrial corrosion science, prevention, monitoring, pipeline integrity, and sustainable surface engineering.

Shashi Bhushan Arya, associate professor in the Department of Metallurgical and Materials Engineering at NIT-K Surathkal, received the Electrochemical Society of India’s S.K. Seshadri Memorial Mascot National Award–2026, with a cash prize of ₹25,000, for work on industrial corrosion science, prevention, monitoring, pipeline integrity, and sustainable surface engineering. The award was presented during the 33rd NSEST–2026 at JRD Tata Auditorium, IISc, Bengaluru, on Friday and Saturday by Shubha V. Iyengar, Padma Shri awardee and former distinguished scientist at CSIR-NAL. He delivered a lecture titled “Flow-Driven Internal Corrosion of Petroleum Pipelines: Mechanisms, Monitoring, and Mitigation.”

**What this is**
- **Concept:** NIT-K associate prof. Shashi Bhushan receives ECSI award
- **Told:** Shashi Bhushan Arya, associate professor in the Department of Metallurgical and Materials Engineering at NIT-K Surathkal, received the Electrochemical Society of India’s S.K.
- **Purpose:** He delivered a lecture titled “Flow-Driven Internal Corrosion of Petroleum Pipelines: Mechanisms, Monitoring, and Mitigation.”

**Important points**
- The award was presented during the 33rd NSEST–2026 at JRD Tata Auditorium, IISc, Bengaluru, on Friday and Saturday by Shubha V.
- Iyengar, Padma Shri awardee and former distinguished scientist at CSIR-NAL.
- He delivered a lecture titled “Flow-Driven Internal Corrosion of Petroleum Pipelines: Mechanisms, Monitoring, and Mitigation.”.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.3; The Hindu Mangaluru p.6_

#### 2. Govt notifies Rs 1,27,500-crore Semicon 2.0 programme

**In short:** The government on Monday notified a Rs 1.27 lakh crore Semicon 2.0 programme, offering fiscal support across the semiconductor value chain including 40% funding for large silicon wafer fabs with a minimum investment threshold of Rs 20,000 crore. The programme, approved by the Union Cabinet in July with an outlay of Rs 1,27,500 crore, covers six pillars and 10 categories spanning chip design, semiconductor equipment and materials, fabrication, packaging, R&D and talent creation, with IT Minister Ashwini Vaishnaw saying the application window is open now.

#### 3. Amaravati to get India's first dedicated univ for quantum tech, AI and other deep-tech

**In short:** Andhra Pradesh has approved India's first dedicated Quantum and Artificial Intelligence University campus in Amaravati, spearheaded by NIELIT and fully funded by MeitY at Rs 730 crore on 8.5 acres in the CRDA region. The campus will offer programmes in quantum computing, AI, semiconductors and other deep-tech areas, train approximately 8,250 learners over five years, and until completion academic programmes will run at Acharya Nagarjuna University in Guntur.

#### 4. Enhancing safety in wilderness

**In short:** Rajeshwari Gopal of Mangaluru writes that the Karnataka Forest Department's decision to make GPS tracking devices compulsory for all trekkers across the state's forest routes is a step in the right direction, as eco-tourism grows on Western Ghats trails such as Kudremukh and Kodachadri where lost hikers and delayed search operations have become recurring concerns. She argues that satellite-enabled tracking ensures real-time monitoring and swift emergency response, though technology alone cannot replace ecological discipline.

#### 5. 6 scientists, 120 research papers, 1 big bet—this IISc startup aims to put India on GaN map

**In short:** Bengaluru startup Agnit Semiconductors, born at IISc's Innovation Centre, has become India's first Gallium Nitride-based semiconductor company with a team of seven researchers and over 15 years of collective GaN experience aiming to design and manufacture GaN-powered semiconductors within five years. CEO Hareesh Chandrasekar said India is not too far behind in GaN technology and can catch up with government incentives under National Semiconductor Mission 2.0's Rs 1,27,500 crore outlay.

### More technology

#### TN govt increases milk procurement prices

**In short:** Tamil Nadu Chief Minister C Joseph Vijay announced in the Assembly on Monday a further hike in Aavin milk procurement prices from Rs 41 to Rs 44 per litre, entailing an additional Rs 60 crore monthly or Rs 720 crore annual outlay, following an earlier hike from Rs 38 to Rs 41 on August 19. Vijay also unveiled major infrastructure investments including Rs 33,066 crore for the Tamil Nadu Transmission Improvement Scheme, a Rs 20,800 crore 1,600 MW super critical thermal plant in Tuticorin, a Rs 175 crore semiconductor park at Maduramangalam, a Rs 400 crore FinTech Hub in Coimbatore and a multi-modal logistics hub in Tiruchirapalli.

#### Can AI help save the planet and help build peace?

**In short:** Deepika Amirapu writes that artificial intelligence can serve as an early-warning system for farmers, traffic, forest fires and floods, connecting Sustainable Development Goals to everyday struggles over water, food and energy in cities such as Bengaluru. She argues AI can analyse climate, food, migration and economic conditions to flag rising risks before crises erupt, though Green AI is essential because data centres consume enormous electricity and water, and educational institutions should encourage students to solve real societal problems with AI.

#### Japan plans defence boost with drones, AI and missiles

**In short:** Japan plans to use drones, AI and new methods of warfare as it adapts its defence strategies, according to details of an 8.9 trillion yen ($55.6 bn) budget request for the coming fiscal year that is only a small increase from this year's 8.8 trillion yen request. Under its five-year defence strategy set in 2022, Japan has doubled its defence spending to about 2% of its gross domestic product, with further details to come after Prime Minister Sanae Takaichi's government approves a new security and defence strategy aimed at strengthening Japan's counterstrike capability.

#### 'Programme to catalyse over Rs 5L crore cumulative investment'

**In short:** Industry leaders and experts have hailed the government's official notification of the Rs 1.27 lakh crore Semicon 2.0 programme, with the India Electronics and Semiconductor Association (IESA) calling it a very significant milestone. IESA President Ashok Chandak said the programme could catalyse more than Rs 5 lakh crore of cumulative private and industry investment over the next five to seven years across fabs, ATMP/OSAT, equipment and materials, design, R&D and the wider supply chain.

#### NASA launches space telescope

**In short:** NASA's Nancy Grace Roman Space Telescope successfully lifted off aboard a SpaceX Falcon Heavy rocket from Kennedy Space Centre on Sunday and separated from the upper stage about 30 minutes later, beginning a 100-day journey to the second Sun-Earth Lagrange point. Equipped with a 2.4-metre primary mirror and 300.8-megapixel Wide Field Instrument with a field of view 100 times larger than Hubble's, Roman could discover up to 100,000 exoplanets during its five-year mission.

Equipped with a 2.4-metre primary mirror and 300.8-megapixel Wide Field Instrument with a field of view 100 times larger than Hubble's, Roman could discover up to 100,000 exoplanets during its five-year mission.

#### ChatGPT 1st AI chatbot to face tougher EU rules

**In short:** ChatGPT will have to comply with tougher safety rules after the European Union added it on Monday to a list of digital services subject to greater legal scrutiny in Europe, a first for an AI chatbot. Brussels also designated the Reddit forum and the popular gaming network Roblox as "very large" online platforms, a label that comes with additional obligations and oversight.

#### TomoClub AI Innovation Challenge for Youth

**In short:** The TomoClub AI Innovation Challenge for Youth is open to students aged 8–15 worldwide, with no coding experience required, asking participants to identify a real-world problem, use AI to build a prototype and explain their solution in a short video. Participants receive a personalised assessment report and certificate, while winners can receive prizes including a Nintendo Switch game, LEGO set, gaming controller or gift card; apply before September 20 at t.ly/_Kh_U.

#### AstroRad vest offers lighter shield against solar storms

**In short:** StemRad, with Lockheed Martin, developed the AstroRad vest using hydrogen-rich polymers to shield bone marrow and organs; NASA tested it on the ISS in 2019–2020 and on Artemis I in 2022 with dummies Helga and Zohar. Results in Science Advances on August 12 found AstroRad could reduce the effective radiation dose of the August 1972 solar storm by 60% and the October 1989 storm by 40%.

StemRad, with Lockheed Martin, developed the AstroRad vest using hydrogen-rich polymers to shield bone marrow and organs; NASA tested it on the ISS in 2019–2020 and on Artemis I in 2022 with dummies Helga and Zohar. Results in Science Advances on August 12 found AstroRad could reduce the effective radiation dose of the August 1972 solar storm by 60% and the October 1989 storm by 40%. The vest cannot block galactic cosmic rays.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** AstroRad vest offers lighter shield against solar storms

## Opinion & Explainers

### Rahul's take on Manusmriti resonates Gandhi, Ambedkar (Opinion)

**In short:** S N Sahu writes that Rahul Gandhi's locating of Manusmriti as the main source of retardation of women's agency at the Pune edition of Chhatron ki Goonj captures a common vision of Mahatma Gandhi and B.R. Ambedkar, echoing Gandhi's 1918 Bhagini Samaj speech and his 1934 rejection of Manu's dictum that women can have no freedom.

- S N Sahu writes that Rahul Gandhi's locating of Manusmriti as the main source of retardation of women's agency at the Pune edition of Chhatron ki Goonj captures a common vision of Mahatma Gandhi and B.R.
- Ambedkar, echoing Gandhi's 1918 Bhagini Samaj speech and his 1934 rejection of Manu's dictum that women can have no freedom.
- Sahu, who served as Officer on Special Duty to President K.R.
- Narayanan, argues Gandhi's critical stand on Smritis and Ambedkar's 1950 article on the rise and fall of the Hindu woman find reflection in Rahul Gandhi's 2026 Pune speech.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.6_

### A Welcome Change (Opinion)

**In short:** Richard Lloyd writes that the RSS chief Mohan Bhagwat's call for oneness during his US tour should be welcomed if it represents a real change of heart for an organisation known for ideology propounding exclusion of religious minorities, citing Bhagwat's New York declaration that "if a Hindu thinks that there should be no Muslims in Bharat, he will not remain a Hindu." Lloyd argues critics would be justified in terming it hypocrisy if meant only for US audiences, noting Muslims and Christians have found public space shrinking since 2019, while Pakistan's coalition government has taken small steps toward reconciliation by restoring Hindu names in Lahore.

- Richard Lloyd writes that the RSS chief Mohan Bhagwat's call for oneness during his US tour should be welcomed if it represents a real change of heart for an organisation known for ideology propounding exclusion of religious minorities, citing Bhagwat's New York declaration that "if a Hindu thinks that there should be no Muslims in Bharat, he will not remain a Hindu." Lloyd argues critics would be justified in terming it hypocrisy if meant only for US audiences, noting Muslims and Christians have found public space shrinking since 2019, while Pakistan's coalition government has taken small steps toward reconciliation by restoring Hindu names in Lahore.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.6_

### Modi in quest for an Eurasian highway (Opinion)

**In short:** Meenakashi Lekhi writes that Prime Minister Narendra Modi's four-day Central Asia visit—including bilateral talks in Uzbekistan and the 26th SCO summit in Bishkek—is important because Central Asia is a gateway to Europe and India needs resilient multi-modal connectivity after geopolitical disruptions in Hormuz and Chabahar compromised traditional Middle East routes. She argues India must build genuine strategic interdependence through trade, technology, healthcare, critical minerals and digital infrastructure, and that Modi's SCO participation aims to create connectivity that cannot be held hostage to another country's geopolitical crisis.

- Meenakashi Lekhi writes that Prime Minister Narendra Modi's four-day Central Asia visit—including bilateral talks in Uzbekistan and the 26th SCO summit in Bishkek—is important because Central Asia is a gateway to Europe and India needs resilient multi-modal connectivity after geopolitical disruptions in Hormuz and Chabahar compromised traditional Middle East routes.
- She argues India must build genuine strategic interdependence through trade, technology, healthcare, critical minerals and digital infrastructure, and that Modi's SCO participation aims to create connectivity that cannot be held hostage to another country's geopolitical crisis.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.6_

### Rebuilding for future floods (Opinion)

**In short:** Writing after the August 26 catastrophic flood along Nepal's Lende-Bhote Koshi system that killed more than 900 people and left over 4,000 missing, retired Geological Survey of India Additional Director General N R Ramesh argues the disaster exposes the Himalayan region's geological fragility and the need for flood-hazard zoning, slope stabilisation and early-warning systems rather than rebuilding alone. He notes India faces downstream risk from barrier lakes on the Tibetan side and from upstream Chinese infrastructure, citing bodies recovered in Uttar Pradesh and concerns over information control and river-flow manipulation near the international boundary.

- Writing after the August 26 catastrophic flood along Nepal's Lende-Bhote Koshi system that killed more than 900 people and left over 4,000 missing, retired Geological Survey of India Additional Director General N R Ramesh argues the disaster exposes the Himalayan region's geological fragility and the need for flood-hazard zoning, slope stabilisation and early-warning systems rather than rebuilding alone.
- He notes India faces downstream risk from barrier lakes on the Tibetan side and from upstream Chinese infrastructure, citing bodies recovered in Uttar Pradesh and concerns over information control and river-flow manipulation near the international boundary.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.8_

### When policy turns, explanation must follow (Opinion)

**In short:** Economist Ajit Ranade uses the Reserve Bank of India's abrupt decision to advance the closing date of the FCNR(B) deposit scheme from September 30 to August 31 — nine days after Governor Sanjay Malhotra said there was no proposal to close it early — to argue that evidence-backed policy reversals are defensible but unexplained U-turns erode institutional credibility. He contends policymakers should pre-specify triggers for course changes and explain what changed on the ground, citing parallels from farm reforms, PLI, IMF programmes and World Bank shifts.

- Economist Ajit Ranade uses the Reserve Bank of India's abrupt decision to advance the closing date of the FCNR(B) deposit scheme from September 30 to August 31 — nine days after Governor Sanjay Malhotra said there was no proposal to close it early — to argue that evidence-backed policy reversals are defensible but unexplained U-turns erode institutional credibility.
- He contends policymakers should pre-specify triggers for course changes and explain what changed on the ground, citing parallels from farm reforms, PLI, IMF programmes and World Bank shifts.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.8_

### Bring in experts (Opinion)

**In short:** In a letter to the editor, Ali Khwaja of Bengaluru writes that Nepal's unfolding tragedy shows nature repeatedly striking communities where massive tourism, mining and power projects proceed unchecked, and that development, ecology and disaster-protection decisions should be taken by experts rather than politicians and bureaucrats. He argues the poor suffer most from such catastrophes, though anyone can be hit without warning.

- In a letter to the editor.
- Ali Khwaja of Bengaluru writes that Nepal's unfolding tragedy shows nature repeatedly striking communities where massive tourism, mining and power projects proceed unchecked, and that development, ecology and disaster-protection decisions should be taken by experts rather than politicians and bureaucrats.
- He argues the poor suffer most from such catastrophes, though anyone can be hit without warning.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.8_
